TSL Bets: The Wednesday parlay (~+600) — Celtics -7.5, Lakers -11.5, Penguins-Sharks Over 6.5

By Matt

Three legs, three different bets: the Celtics covering in Toronto, the Lakers rolling a one-win Bucks team, and a goals total in San Jose. One big spread, one smaller one, one total — about +600.

Leg 1: Celtics -7.5 at Raptors

Boston Celtics (9-2) at Toronto Raptors (5-5) — 7:00 PM ET, Scotiabank Arena

Celtics -7.5 (-110), total 215.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Boston: Nobody on the injury report. The rotation is fully healthy.

Injuries — Toronto: Scottie Barnes is out with a groin injury (third straight game), and Kawhi Leonard is out with a knee injury (out at least another week). Immanuel Quickley is questionable with a hamstring injury, and Collin Murray-Boyles and Trayce Jackson-Davis are questionable with illnesses.

Storylines: The Celtics have won four straight and are going for a fifth, after beating the 76ers 123-101 on Tuesday — Jaylen Brown scored 36 and Jayson Tatum added 21 points, 6 rebounds and 4 assists. Toronto is missing its two best players. The caveat is real: Boston is on the second night of a back-to-back, and there was a note earlier in the week that Brown might get a rest day on Wednesday — he played Tuesday after resting Monday, so the rest risk is worth naming. But the Raptors without Barnes and Kawhi are a different team, and the Celtics are healthy.

Leg 2: Lakers -11.5 at Bucks

Los Angeles Lakers (10-2) at Milwaukee Bucks (1-10) — 8:00 PM ET, Fiserv Forum

Lakers -11.5 (-110), total 232.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Los Angeles: Austin Reaves is back — off the injury report after the illness that kept him out Monday. Luka Doncic is clear. Quentin Grimes is questionable with a right finger sprain.

Injuries — Milwaukee: Tyler Herro is out with a right knee contusion — and he is the Bucks' leading scorer at 21.4 points a game.

Storylines: The Bucks are 1-10. They have lost six straight, and they lost to the Knicks 117-85 on Tuesday. The Lakers are 10-2, on a six-game win streak, and they just won in Atlanta without Reaves. This is the parlay's one big spread — the Lakers are the only favorite laying more than 8.5 — and it is the leg with the most room to be wrong, because 11.5 points on the road is a big number. But the Bucks are missing their leading scorer, they are losing by double digits most nights, and the Lakers are the best team in the league right now.

Leg 3: Penguins at Sharks Over 6.5

Pittsburgh Penguins (6-10-2) at San Jose Sharks (8-11-1) — 10:00 PM ET, SAP Center

Penguins -125, total 6.5, Over -110 (DraftKings via ESPN). Projected goalies: Arturs Silovs (PIT) vs Yaroslav Askarov (SJ).

Injuries — Pittsburgh: Bryan Rust is on IR with a lower-body injury, Ryan Graves is on IR with a knee injury, and Elmer Soderblom is on IR. Ville Koivunen is day-to-day with a lower-body injury, and James van Riemsdyk is on IR.

Injuries — San Jose: Igor Chernyshov is on IR, Quentin Musty is on IR-NR, and Michael Keyser is on IR. All depth.

Storylines: Macklin Celebrini is at 199 career points in 170 games (77 goals, 122 assists) and has 21 points in 18 games this season — he took six shots on Monday against the Islanders and came away with no points, so he is due. The goalie matchup is two young netminders, Silovs and Askarov, and both teams leak goals — Pittsburgh is 29th in the NHL, San Jose 25th. Rudebets projects a 6.86 total for this game. The Over needs seven goals, and these two teams give up enough of them.

The bet

Parlay: Celtics -7.5 (-110) + Lakers -11.5 (-110) + Penguins-Sharks Over 6.5 (-110) — about +600.

One big spread, one smaller spread, one total. That is the whole point of the construction: the Lakers are the only leg laying more than 8.5, and the other two legs are built on different kinds of bets — a healthy favorite covering a manageable number, and a goals total between two leaky teams. None of these legs are copies of the single-game bets today, and each one clears on its own merits.

The honest caveat

The Lakers are the risk. Eleven and a half points is a big number on the road, and if the Bucks keep it close — or if the Lakers take their foot off the gas in the fourth — the whole ticket dies on the second leg. The Celtics are on a back-to-back with a rest risk on their best player, and the Over needs seven goals from two teams that can both go quiet. A parlay is a parlay: three things have to go right, and one of them is a double-digit road spread. Bet accordingly.

The pick

Celtics -7.5 + Lakers -11.5 + Penguins-Sharks Over 6.5 — about +600. A $10 ticket returns about $70. One big spread, one smaller one, one total — three different bets, one ticket.

TSL Bets: Oilers -1.5 (+124) vs Flyers — McDavid and Draisaitl against a tired Flyers team

By Matt

The Oilers are 13-3-3 with the league's hottest two players, and the Flyers are 5-10-5 with two defensemen out. Edmonton on the puck line at plus money is the play tonight.

The game

Philadelphia Flyers (5-10-5) at Edmonton Oilers (13-3-3) — 9:00 PM ET, Rogers Place, Edmonton, ESPN+

Oilers -1.5 (+124), moneyline Oilers -218, total 6.5 (DraftKings via ESPN). Projected goalies: Dan Vladar (PHI) vs Devon Levi (EDM).

Injuries — Edmonton: Depth only. Matthew Savoie is on IR with a lower-body injury, Andersen is on IR with a knee injury, and Mattias Janmark, Isaac Howard and Spencer Regula are all on IR as well. None of them are core skaters — the rotation is intact.

Injuries — Philadelphia: Rasmus Ristolainen is out with a hand injury and Nikita Grebenkin is out with a back injury. The Flyers are thinner on the blue line against the best offense in hockey.

Storylines: Connor McDavid has 48 points (11 goals, 37 assists) and a season-opening point streak of 19 games — the fourth player ever to start a season with a 19-game point streak. Leon Draisaitl leads the league with 18 goals and has scored in each of his last four games, six goals in that stretch, including the last goal in each of the last four. The Oilers are first in the Pacific at 13-3-3. The Flyers, meanwhile, lost 4-3 in overtime at Calgary on Sunday and are 5-10-5. Devon Levi, getting the start for Edmonton, stopped 29 of 30 on Monday against the Rangers.

The bet

Oilers -1.5 (+124). This is the best offense in the league, at home, against a Flyers team that is 5-10-5 and missing two defensemen. The puck line at plus money is the play — you are getting a two-goal margin on a team that wins by two or more more often than not, and the price says the market thinks this is closer than it is. McDavid and Draisaitl are playing at a level where the Flyers' depleted blue line has no answer.

The honest caveat

The puck line is the risk. Edmonton can win this game 4-3 and the ticket loses — the -1.5 needs a two-goal margin, not just a win. The Flyers have kept games close even while losing, and Levi is a young goalie getting a second start in three nights. And McDavid's 19-game point streak has to end sometime — if tonight is the night, the offense has to find another way. This is a plus-money bet for a reason. But the Oilers are the better team by a wide margin, and the price is right.

The pick

Oilers -1.5 (+124). A $10 ticket returns about $22.40. The best offense in hockey, at home, against a 5-10-5 team, at plus money.

TSL Bets: Warriors +6.5 (-110) at Rockets — eight straight wins and you're giving me points?

By Matt

The Warriors have won eight straight after an 0-3 start, Steph Curry is averaging 30.6 a game, and Houston is laying 6.5 with a banged-up frontcourt. Take the points with the hottest team in the league.

The game

Golden State Warriors (8-3) at Houston Rockets (8-3) — 9:30 PM ET, Toyota Center, ESPN

Rockets -6.5 (-110), moneyline Rockets -237, total 222.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Golden State: Jimmy Butler is out with a knee injury, Moses Moody is out with a knee injury, Gary Payton II is out with a knee injury, and Brandon Williams is out with a hamstring injury. Will Richard is questionable with a thumb injury (UCL tear). Kristaps Porzingis is away from the team. The Warriors are shorthanded — but they have been shorthanded all through this win streak.

Injuries — Houston: Clint Capela is out with an ankle injury and Steven Adams is out with a calf injury, leaving Alperen Sengun as the only healthy center. Oscar Tshiebwe is questionable with an illness. The Rockets' expected starters are Fred VanVleet, Amen Thompson, Kevin Durant, Jabari Smith Jr. and Sengun.

Storylines: The Warriors started 0-3 and have won eight straight since — the first team in NBA history to open a season with three losses and then win eight in a row. Steph Curry is averaging 30.6 points a game on 45.7% from three, and 32.3 over his last five. This is also a Durant-versus-Curry night — the former teammates who built a dynasty together, now on opposite sides of a matchup between two 8-3 teams. Both teams played Monday and had one day of rest. Houston's frontcourt depth is the question: with Capela and Adams out, Sengun is doing the center job alone against a Warriors team that pushes the pace.

The bet

Warriors +6.5 (-110). Six and a half points for a team that has not lost in eight games, with the best shooter alive playing the best basketball of his season. The Warriors are banged up, yes — but they have been banged up through the entire streak, and they keep winning anyway. Houston is good, and the Rockets are favored for a reason, but 6.5 is a lot of points against a team that has covered at a high rate during this run. Take the points.

The honest caveat

The Warriors are genuinely shorthanded — Butler, Moody and Payton are all out, and Richard is questionable. If Curry has an off night, there is not much behind him to carry the scoring. And the Rockets are 8-3 with Durant, VanVleet and Thompson, at home, with the better frontcourt. Houston could win this game by 10 and nobody would be surprised. The +6.5 is a bet on the streak and the shooter, not on the matchup — and streaks end. But the number is the number, and it is generous for a team that has not lost in a month.

The pick

Warriors +6.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $19.09. Eight straight wins, Steph Curry at 30.6 a game, and you're giving me six and a half points.

By Matt

Knicks -4.5 (-110), moneyline Knicks -166, total 221.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Collectibles · Nov 11, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

Burger King is selling a Magic: The Gathering deck box with your Whopper — Italy only

By Matt

Burger King Italy has launched 'The Great Gathering' — a €19.90 bundle that comes with a rigid burger-styled Magic deck box and 100 sleeves. No promo cards, but nine restaurants are running Pauper tournaments with a final at Milan Games Week.

The bundle

Magic: The Gathering has done a lot of crossovers. A fast-food one was not on the list until this week.

Burger King Italy has launched "Burger King Quest: The Great Gathering," a bundle that runs from November 10 through November 30 at participating Italian locations. The price is €19.90 — about $22 — and it gets you a choice of sandwich, fries and a drink, plus the thing everyone is actually here for: a rigid Burger King x Magic deck box, styled to look like a burger, and a pack of 100 card sleeves.

The sandwich is your choice of the Chicken Krispper, the Double Cheese King, or a plant-based Whopper. The box itself is the prize — an orange, sesame-seed-dotted deck box with the Burger King logo on one side and the Magic logo on the other — and Polygon reports it is already being resold online, with scalpers flipping the box before the promotion is even halfway through.

There are no promo cards in the bundle. The accessories are the product.

The tournaments

This is not just a meal with a toy in it. Nine Burger King restaurants across Italy are hosting Pauper tournaments as part of the promotion — competitive Magic in the cheap, commons-only format — with qualifiers running through the month and a final at Milan Games Week & Cartoomics, November 27–29.

It is a real competitive structure wrapped around a fast-food promotion, which is exactly why it is working. The Pauper community gets a tournament circuit with a national final; Burger King gets a month of people coming through the door for something they cannot get at a card shop.

Why it matters

Wizards of the Coast has never done a fast-food partnership like this. The closest comparison is Pokémon's Happy Meal runs, and those move staggering numbers — the recent U.S. Pokémon Happy Meal launch had McDonald's locations selling out within days. Burger King is betting the same energy works for an older, tournament-playing audience, and the resale market says they were right to try.

The math is worth noting, too. A regular Burger King meal in Italy runs around €11.50. The bundle at €19.90 means the deck box and sleeves are effectively costing about €8.40 — less than most standalone deck boxes, and this one looks like a burger.

The catch, of course, is that it is Italy only. No U.S. dates, no wider European rollout announced. If you want one, you either know someone in Milan or you pay the reseller price — which, given how this promotion is going, is not going down.

Gaming · Nov 11, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

Ubisoft makes it official: the Tubarão-Noor combo is banned for the rest of the Osaka Major

By Matt

Ubisoft has ruled on the Rainbow Six Siege combo that dominated the BLAST R6 Major Osaka's opening days: the Tubarão-Noor gadget interaction is banned until the end of the event, and any team that uses it loses the round.

The ruling

The BLAST R6 Major Osaka had a problem before it really got started, and on Wednesday Ubisoft finally put it in writing.

The interaction between Tubarão's Zoto Canister and Noor's Horus Lance is banned from now until the end of the event. Any team confirmed using it loses the round. The ruling is not retroactive — results from the opening days stand — and neither operator is banned. Tubarão and Noor are still playable; their gadgets just cannot be used together anymore.

The ruling comes under section 4.6 of the Osaka Major rulebook, and it closes a saga that began the moment the tournament opened.

How it worked

Here is the combo: Tubarão's Zoto Canister freezes both a Horus Lance and a hard breach gadget. When the freeze ends, the Lance disables the gadget it was touching. The result is a hard-breach denial that is, on paper, perfectly legal — and brutally effective.

The players knew what it was the second they saw it. On the tournament's opening day, teams raised it with BLAST, and BLAST confirmed with Ubisoft that the interaction was intentional — not a bug. That is where the trouble started. If Ubisoft knew the interaction existed, the players wanted to know why nobody said anything before the Major began. The patch notes said nothing. The teams found out on stage.

The gentlemen's agreement

Four days ago, the players took it into their own hands. All twenty teams at the Major agreed — a voluntary gentlemen's agreement, announced publicly by Team Liquid in-game leader Felipe "FelipoX" Lucia on November 7 — not to use the interaction at all. It was, by most accounts, the first agreement of its kind in Rainbow Six Siege esports in years.

Now the agreement is a rule. Ubisoft's official ruling, published Wednesday, matches the players' position almost exactly: banned from November 11 until the end of the event, round loss for confirmed use, operators still playable separately.

It is a strange way for a major to be decided — the players agreed on the competitive integrity of the tournament before the publisher did — but the important part is that the question is settled before the weekend. The grand final is this weekend, and the Season 4 update gets its reveal there too. The players can finally go back to playing the game instead of arguing about it.

Kai Cenat says he's 'genuinely terrified of human beings' as the Clover Boys fallout spills over

By Matt

Dezz says Kai Cenat cut off the Clover Boys. Kai answered on his GTA marathon stream — then admitted on camera that he's 'so scared to meet new people.' Here's what we actually know.

What happened

The Clover Boys are the group Kai Cenat built — Dezz, Tylil, Punga, RaKai, Ray and Reggie, a streaming circle that goes back to college. This week, one of its founders said it was falling apart in public.

Dezz hosted a TikTok livestream on November 9 and accused Kai of cutting off communication with him and the other Clover Boys streamers. His line, as it made the rounds: Kai "don't speak to none of us gang." He also said Kaiya Cenat — Kai's sister — had stopped talking to him as well.

Kai addressed it the same day, during his GTA marathon stream. He was not having it. He told his chat he was "tired of the bull**" and that people should "leave him alone" — the kind of answer that says more by what it does not say than by what it does.

Then, on November 10, a different clip started circulating — shared by X user @scubaryan_ — where Kai talked about why he pulls back from people at all. "I'm genuinely terrified of human beings," he said. "I'm so scared to meet new people." It was not a response to Dezz, exactly. It was the context underneath the whole thing.

Why this one stings

Kai and Dezz go back further than most people in this scene. They met in college, and Dezz helped produce Kai's early videos — he was there before the fame, before the numbers, before the marathon streams. The Clover Boys were supposed to be the answer to the lonely-at-the-top problem: a group that grew together instead of one star outgrowing everyone around him.

But the fractures have been visible for a while. Reggie distanced himself from the group in September, and Kai said in October that he has invested $3.7 million into the Clover Boys. When the money and the distance both get that big, a group of friends starts to look like a business — and businesses have arguments that friendships do not survive.

What we don't know

The important part: neither Kai nor Kaiya has confirmed Dezz's account of being cut off, and the full picture is murkier than one livestream makes it sound. Kai streamed with Ray in Japan recently, which cuts against the idea that he has shut everyone out. The "cut off everyone" claim, in other words, is one person's telling of it — and Kai's own words on stream read less like a denial of the distance and more like an explanation of why it exists.

This is not a story with a villain. It is a story about a streamer who got famous faster than anyone in his circle expected, and a group that is finding out what that does to a friendship.

Dawson Mercer burns his old team as Jets beat Devils 4-2 in Hellebuyck's return to Winnipeg

By Matt

Dawson Mercer scored twice against the team that traded him — including the power-play winner — and Nico Daws got the win in the other net as Winnipeg beat New Jersey 4-2 on the night Connor Hellebuyck came home and never played.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Tuesday in Winnipeg was supposed to be about Connor Hellebuyck. The former Jets goalie came back to Canada Life Centre for the first time since his trade request was granted on October 23, and the building was ready for him — the fans booed him like he had started all 60 minutes.

He never played a second. The Devils went with Jake Allen in net, and the two players New Jersey sent the other way in the deal decided the game instead.

Dawson Mercer scored twice against his former team, including the tiebreaking power-play goal with 8:02 left in the third period, and Nico Daws — the other half of the return package — made 19 saves for his first win as a Jet as Winnipeg beat New Jersey 4-2. Kyle Connor scored twice as well, adding an empty-netter with 58 seconds left to seal it.

The goals told the story of the night. Connor put Winnipeg up 2-0 in the first. Jesper Bratt and Cody Glass answered 44 seconds apart early in the second to tie it, and the building went quiet for a while. Then the Devils took a too-many-men penalty in the third, Mercer buried the power play at 11:58, and the Jets had their fourth straight win — this one after a nine-game skid that had buried their season before it started.

The trade, in one night

This is what a revenge game is supposed to look like. Mercer and Daws were not bit players in the Hellebuyck deal — they were the package — and on the night Hellebuyck returned to Winnipeg, they were the two best Jets on the ice. Mercer has now scored three goals since changing teams. Daws outdueled Allen, who made 21 saves in the loss.

Hellebuyck, for his part, watched from the bench as the crowd chanted at him. Devils general manager Sunny Mehta had announced on Sunday that Hellebuyck would not start, and head coach Scott Arniel said afterward that he hopes the win brings closure for his team — the Jets still have to go to New Jersey on November 28, but the home half of the saga is done.

The loss ended New Jersey's five-game winning streak and dropped the Devils to 13-6-0. Winnipeg improved to 8-8-3, and after nine straight losses, four straight wins has them back in the conversation.

The bigger picture

The Jets spent the first month of the season in a hole of their own making. Four straight wins does not erase a nine-game skid, but beating the team that took your franchise goalie — with the two players you got back doing the damage — is the kind of night that changes how a room feels about itself.

Winnipeg visits Dallas on Thursday. New Jersey hosts Washington the same night.

Luka Doncic returns with 32, 8 and 10 as Lakers hold off Hawks for sixth straight win

By Matt

Luka Doncic came back from a one-game absence with 32 points, 8 rebounds and 10 assists as the Lakers beat the Hawks 124-114 in Atlanta — their sixth straight win, even with Austin Reaves sidelined.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Luka Doncic missed one game. The Lakers did not miss a beat.

Back in the lineup after sitting out Sunday in Detroit with lower-back tightness, Doncic put up 32 points, 8 rebounds, 10 assists, 5 steals and 2 blocks in 39 minutes as Los Angeles beat the Atlanta Hawks 124-114 at State Farm Arena on Monday night. The win is the Lakers' sixth in a row, and it pushes them to 10-2 — the best record in the league.

He did it the way he has been doing it all season: everywhere. Doncic shot 11-of-22 from the field and 7-of-7 from the line, had 8 assists before halftime, and scored 18 of his points after the break as Atlanta kept charging. The Hawks cut a double-digit lead to 115-111 with about three and a half minutes left, and the Lakers still closed it out — their bench doing most of the closing.

That bench was the story of the night. With Austin Reaves out with an illness one night after his season-high 43 in Detroit, the Lakers' reserves scored a season-high 49 points. Sandro Mamukelashvili led the way with 23 points, 8 rebounds and 3 assists in his best game as a Laker. A night after the bench put up 45 in Detroit without Doncic, it put up 49 without Reaves. Coach JJ Redick praised the depth afterward, and he had every reason to.

The streak ends

The one thing Doncic did not do: score 40. His run of four straight 40-point games ended in Atlanta, eight points short of a fifth — the number that would have put him alongside Wilt Chamberlain, Kobe Bryant, Michael Jordan, Allen Iverson, Elgin Baylor and James Harden as the only players in NBA history with five consecutive 40-point games.

He will live with it. The Lakers are 10-2, he still leads the league in scoring at just over 39 points a game, and he was named Western Conference Player of the Week on Monday after averaging 45.0 points, 7.0 rebounds and 8.3 assists across a 3-0 week. The scoring streak was fun. The winning streak matters more, and it is still alive.

Atlanta, meanwhile, saw its own four-game winning streak end. The Hawks had a real chance late and could not finish, and now they watch the Lakers head to Milwaukee on Wednesday with Reaves expected back and Quentin Grimes — out Monday with a sprained right finger — listed as questionable.

The draft-night troll

There was one more moment worth keeping. Doncic was drafted third overall in 2018 by Atlanta, then traded on draft night to Dallas for Trae Young and a protected first-round pick. On Monday, with the game in hand, he had words with a Hawks fan in the crowd — the clip made the rounds within the hour — needling the home fans about the team that drafted him and sent him away.

Eight years later, the trade is still one of the most second-guessed deals in modern NBA history, and Doncic is still happy to remind Atlanta of it in person.

Celtics dominate 76ers in first Tatum-Brown meeting as Paul George torches his old team

By Matt

The summer trade's grudge match went to Boston — and the new guy in green stole the show.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The game everyone had circled since the summer ended the way Boston drew it up. The Celtics beat the 76ers 123-101 in Philadelphia on Tuesday night, in the first regular-season meeting since the blockbuster that sent Jaylen Brown to the Sixers and Paul George to Boston — and they never trailed. The headliner was supposed to be Jayson Tatum against his former running mate. The story turned out to be the other guy in the trade.

The night belonged to George

George went off against the team that dealt him away, pouring in a game-high 36 points on 14-of-22 shooting, including 6-of-11 from three, with 5 rebounds. He did his loudest damage late: the broadcast graphic during the run said it plainly — an 18-3 Celtics burst over 5:21 that turned a game into a rout. Every time Philadelphia threatened to make it interesting, the ball found George, and the ball went in.

This was the revenge-game script in its purest form. George spent the summer as the footnote to the Brown-Tatum storyline, the other piece of the deal. In his first game back in Philadelphia wearing green, he was the best player on the floor by a distance, smiling all the way through it. He'd told reporters before shootaround that it feels like "a regular game." It did not look like one.

Round one: Tatum over Brown

Tatum got his win over his old running mate, finishing with 21 points, 6 rebounds, and 4 assists, plus a third-quarter poster dunk on LeBron James that had the Boston bench on its feet. The two spent nine seasons together in Boston, won a championship together, and became the identity of the franchise — then the trade split them up, and the week leading in had all the makings of a feud. The scoreboard backed Tatum up.

Brown's first game against his old team was a quiet one: 13 points on 4-of-9 shooting, 5 rebounds, 2 assists, 4 turnovers, and a minus-27 in 26 minutes. He never found a rhythm, and by the fourth quarter he was watching from the bench as the lead stretched past 20. The building that used to cheer George spent the night watching him cook his old team.

Philly's side of the ledger

Tyrese Maxey kept the Sixers in it as long as anyone could, leading Philadelphia with 24 points on 8-of-12 shooting. Joel Embiid added 16, but needed 13 trips to the line to get there on a 2-of-9 night from the field. And LeBron James finished two boards and five dimes short of a triple-double, closing on 11 points, 8 rebounds, and 5 assists — the watch that had some life in the fourth quarter never got there.

Boston's bench buried them

The starters set the tone, but the bench put the game away. Payton Pritchard came off the bench for 18 points, 6 rebounds, and 5 assists and was a plus-26; Sam Hauser knocked down four threes for 12; Hugo Gonzalez added 10. Philadelphia's best stretch was the third quarter, the only frame it won — and even that only trimmed two points off a double-digit halftime deficit. Boston won the other three quarters going away.

What it means

The winner was always going to take sole possession of first place in the East, and now that belongs to Boston alone: the Celtics sit at 9-2, the Sixers at 9-3. It is one game in November, and the standings will move a dozen times before spring — but the trade's grudge match was not close out of the gate, and the team that supposedly lost its second star looks better for the swap so far. Boston doesn't wait long to build on it: the Celtics are in Toronto tomorrow night, Wednesday, November 11, at 7:00 PM ET against the Raptors. The rematch with Philly isn't until Jan. 21, at TD Garden.

Gaming · Nov 10, 2026 · 9:09 PM ET

ARC Raiders passes 17 million copies sold

By Matt

ARC Raiders has sold more than 17 million copies, Nexon said in its third-quarter earnings release — up from 16.3 million at the end of June, with the October Frozen Trail update pulling lapsed players back a full year after launch.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

ARC Raiders has sold more than 17 million copies. Nexon put the total in its third-quarter earnings release on November 10, 2026, and credited the October Frozen Trail update for pulling lapsed players back a full year after launch.

The numbers

Copies sold to date: More than 17 million

Copies sold at the end of June 2026: 16.3 million

Copies sold by May 2026: 16 million

Matches played in year one: Six billion

Average raids per day in year one: 16 million

Frozen Trail did the work

Nexon's earnings letter calls Frozen Trail, released by Embark Studios on October 8, "the largest update since the 2025 launch" — and says it was "successful in re-engaging lapsed players and driving incremental unit sales." That is the story behind the new total: the game did not just hold its audience, it won back players who had drifted away and sold new copies to players coming in fresh.

The pace tells the rest of it. The gap between the May figure and this one is roughly a million copies over about six months, after the launch rush did the heavy lifting in the first seven. Nexon also said third-quarter revenue from the game came in within its outlook, and that it expects fourth-quarter revenue to stay broadly flat against the third quarter while falling year over year — an honest comparison, since the same quarter last year was launch season.

What a year looks like

The game launched October 30, 2025, on PC, PlayStation 5, and Xbox Series X|S. By its first anniversary it had six billion matches played — an average of 16 million raids a day — and Nexon said in August it had generated more than 88 billion yen since launch. It is a buy-to-play game, so every active player is a paying player, which makes the retention story matter more than it would for a free-to-play title: nobody is padding the numbers with free downloads.

A year after launch, the question for an extraction shooter is never whether the launch was big. It is whether anyone is still raiding. Frozen Trail answered that, and the sales total is the receipt.

NHL · Nov 10, 2026 · 12:27 PM ET

The Chase, Ep. 1: Crosby is 24 points from tying Ron Francis for fifth all-time

By Matt

Sidney Crosby sits at 1,774 career points after passing Marcel Dionne on Saturday in Los Angeles. Ron Francis, fifth all-time at 1,798, is 24 points away from a tie — 25 from being passed. This is The Chase, and we're tracking it every step of the way.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The Chase is on

Sidney Crosby has 1,774 career points. Ron Francis, fifth on the NHL's all-time scoring list, has 1,798.

That's the whole story, and it's the only story that matters for the next few months: Crosby is 24 points from tying Francis, 25 from passing him. Every game from here is a step on that count, and this is the series that's going to track it — episode by episode, point by point, until the night he gets there.

Welcome to The Chase.

How he got here

Saturday night in Los Angeles, Crosby passed Marcel Dionne for sixth all-time. It wasn't a pretty goal — Evgeni Malkin's one-timer from the right circle caught a piece of Crosby on its way in at 17:13 of the second period, and the goal was first credited to Malkin before the replay showed the deflection. But it counted all the same: career point No. 1,772, past Dionne's 1,771, in the building where Dionne did most of his damage across 12 seasons as a King.

Crosby didn't know he'd been credited with it at first. He tried to steer the credit back to Malkin. That's the kind of night it was — and then, with 1:16 left in the third and the Kings' net empty, he dove to poke a loose puck home after being impeded by Erik Gustafsson on the breakaway. Point No. 1,773. Penguins 5, Kings 2. Four-game losing streak over, seven-game road skid over.

Sunday in Anaheim, he added one more: an assist in the Penguins' 5-2 loss to the Ducks. That makes it 1,774 — 660 goals and 1,114 assists in 1,438 games.

He tied Dionne at 1,771 in Montreal on October 31. It took him a week to pass him. He is 39 years old, in his 21st season, and he is still doing this.

The list ahead of him

The names left in front of Crosby are the ones on the building. Gretzky's 2,857 is untouchable, and Gordie Howe's 1,850 is a long way off yet. But Francis, at 1,798, is close enough to watch in real time.

Here's the math, straight off the pace: Crosby has 13 points in 18 games this season — 6 goals, 7 assists, 0.72 points per game. At that pace, the gap closes in roughly 33 games. There are 64 games left on the schedule. If he stays healthy and keeps producing anything like this, he doesn't just catch Francis this season — he clears him with room to spare.

That's pace math, not a prediction. Hockey doesn't work in straight lines, and a cold week or a missed game moves the date. But the direction is clear, and the gap is small enough that every point from here matters.

What it means

Crosby has been chasing history for so long that it's easy to forget how rare this is. Sixth all-time in scoring, in a league that's been around for more than a century, at 39, on a Penguins team that's rebuilding around him. Dionne held sixth place for decades. Crosby took it from him in a week.

And now there's one more name. Francis played 23 seasons and finished with 1,798 points. The next time the Penguins play, the count starts moving again.

What's next

The Penguins are in San Jose on Wednesday night — 10:00 PM ET against the Sharks at SAP Center — then back home Saturday at 7:00 PM ET against Ottawa. Every point Crosby picks up in those games comes off the gap.

We'll be here for all of it. The Chase continues Wednesday.

The box score

Final score (Nov 7): Penguins 5, Kings 2

Final score (Nov 8): Ducks 5, Penguins 2

Crosby: 1,774 career points (660 G, 1,114 A, 1,438 GP)

Milestone: Passed Marcel Dionne (1,771) for 6th all-time, Nov 7

Next target: Ron Francis, 1,798 (5th) — 24 to tie, 25 to pass

This season: 6 G, 7 A, 13 PTS in 18 GP (0.72 PPG)

Next game: Wed Nov 11, 10:00 PM ET at San Jose Sharks

Then: Sat Nov 14, 7:00 PM ET vs Ottawa Senators

NBA · Nov 10, 2026 · 11:11 AM ET

Celtics vs. 76ers preview: Tatum vs. Brown, nine years as teammates, one summer blockbuster later

By Matt

The top two teams in the East meet in Philadelphia tonight, and it's personal: Jaylen Brown faces the Celtics for the first time since the summer trade that sent him to the 76ers and Paul George to Boston.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The matchup

Game: Boston Celtics (8-2) at Philadelphia 76ers (9-2)

Date and time: Tuesday, November 10, 2026, 8:00 PM ET

Arena: Xfinity Mobile Arena, Philadelphia

TV: NBC, Peacock, NBC Sports Boston

The two best teams in the Eastern Conference meet tonight, and the standings are only half the story. This is the first regular-season meeting since the summer blockbuster that split up one of the league's defining duos: Jaylen Brown is a 76er now, Paul George is a Celtic, and Jayson Tatum and Brown — teammates for nine years — share a court for the first time as opponents.

The trade that changed both teams

Brown and Tatum spent nine seasons together in Boston. They grew up in the league side by side, won a championship together, and became the identity of the Celtics. Then the summer trade sent Brown to Philadelphia and brought George to Boston, and the split has not been quiet.

The two haven't talked since the deal. Brown has taken shots at Tatum on social media and in front of reporters, and he has said he does not plan to talk to Tatum on Tuesday — he's treating it like any other game. Tatum, for his part, has kept it flat: "nothing more than Celtics vs. Sixers."

That is not how anyone else will be watching it. Every possession between them will be clipped, every stare will be read into, and the first time Brown and Tatum guard each other will be the moment the whole night is built around. Whatever they say, this one is personal — it just depends on whose version of "personal" shows up on the floor.

The stakes

Philadelphia is 9-2 and sitting first in the East, 4-1 at home after beating the Bulls 127-121 on Sunday. Boston is 8-2 and second, 3-2 on the road, riding a three-game win streak after a 117-97 win over the Bucks. The winner takes sole possession of first place, and in a conference this tight in November, that is not a throwaway line — it's the early-season statement game.

The numbers

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, 6.0 rebounds, and 7.8 assists, and he has found his range from deep after a rocky start — five threes in each of his last two games. George, his new running mate, is at 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, and 4.1 assists in his first weeks in green.

On the other side, Tyrese Maxey leads the Sixers at 26.9 points and 5.5 assists, and Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds, and 4.0 assists in his new uniform. The backcourt battle — Maxey against Boston's guards — and the Brown-Tatum matchup will decide this one, but the quiet swing factor is on the glass: Brown's rebounding from the wing has been one of the early surprises of his Philadelphia stint.

Probable starters

Philadelphia: Tyrese Maxey, VJ Edgecombe, Jaylen Brown, LeBron James, Joel Embiid

Boston: Mike Conley, Derrick White, Paul George, Jayson Tatum, Neemias Queta

Injury report

Celtics: None. Boston is at full strength for the trip to Philadelphia.

76ers: Dominick Barlow (low back stress reaction), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tear) are all out. Joel Embiid is clear to play — the plantar fasciitis in his foot is being managed, and he played through a minutes restriction earlier in the month.

Three storylines to watch

1. The first Tatum-Brown possession. It will happen early, it will be deliberate, and it will set the tone. If Brown comes out hunting the matchup, the building will be on its feet. If Tatum takes it the other way and attacks him, same thing.

2. George's first game against his old team — the other side of the trade. Everyone is watching Brown, but George is playing the team that traded him away, too. A big night from him in Philadelphia would be its own statement, and Boston's offense runs through the Tatum-George two-man game now.

3. Embiid's health, quietly. He is cleared to play and the minutes restriction is behind him, but a 9-2 start built on his availability is the Sixers' whole season. Against Queta and Boston's front line, how he holds up over four quarters matters more than any single highlight.

How to watch

Tipoff is 8:00 PM ET at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia, on NBC, Peacock, and NBC Sports Boston.

TSL Bets: Wild at Canadiens Over 6.5 (-110) — two hot offenses, depleted blue lines, backup goalies

By Matt

Montreal has a point in ten straight and is scoring 3.8 a game in that stretch. Minnesota's offense runs through Kaprizov. Both teams are down key defensemen and goalies. Over 6.5 (-110) is the play in Montreal.

The game

Minnesota Wild (12-3-4, 28 points) at Montreal Canadiens (11-3-3, 25 points) — 7:00 PM ET, Centre Bell

Wild -125 moneyline, total 6.5 (DraftKings, as of November 10, 04:04 ET). Two of the best records in the league, and two offenses that are rolling.

Injuries — Minnesota: Filip Gustavsson is on injured reserve with a hip injury, Jared Spurgeon is on injured reserve with a lower-body injury, Brock Faber is on injured reserve with a skull injury, and Michael McCarron is day-to-day with an upper-body injury. The Wild are down their starting goalie and two of their top defensemen.

Injuries — Montreal: Ivan Demidov is on injured reserve with an ankle injury, Mike Matheson is out with a lower-body injury, Samuel Montembeault is day-to-day with a lower-body injury, Jake Evans is day-to-day with a hand injury, and Josh Anderson is day-to-day for personal reasons. The Canadiens are down a top defenseman and their starting goalie is questionable.

Storylines: Montreal has a point in ten straight games and is averaging 3.8 goals a game in that stretch. Minnesota's offense runs through Kirill Kaprizov, and the Wild have been scoring with anyone. Both teams are missing key defensemen and goalies — the blue lines are depleted and the creases are backup territory. When two hot offenses meet two patched-up defenses, the total is where the value is.

The bet

Wild at Canadiens Over 6.5 (-110). Two top offenses, depleted blue lines, backup goalies on both sides, and a Montreal team that has scored 3.8 a game for ten straight. The total is 6.5 for a reason — it's a high number — but the conditions are exactly the ones that produce seven-goal games. Take the over.

The honest caveat

Six and a half is a high number, and high numbers lose more often than people think. If either backup goalie gets hot, or if the game tightens up in the third period with both teams protecting a lead, this can land on six and burn the ticket. And both teams are good enough defensively at full strength that the depleted versions still aren't pushovers. The over is the right side, but it's a sweat — expect the seventh goal to come late, if it comes at all.

The pick

Wild at Canadiens Over 6.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $19.09. Two hot offenses, two patched-up defenses, and a total that the game script supports.

TSL Bets: Heat -9.5, Magic -5.5, Devils-Jets Over 6 — the Tuesday parlay, about +600

By Matt

Heat -9.5 at the 1-9 Jazz, Magic -5.5 at the 1-9 Kings, and Devils-Jets Over 6. Three legs at -110, about +600. One big spread, two more that stand on their own.

The legs

Leg 1 — Miami Heat -9.5 (-110) at Utah Jazz. Heat (4-6) at Jazz (1-9), 9:00 PM ET, Delta Center. Miami -9.5, total 228.5. The Jazz have lost eight straight. Keyonte George is out with an ankle injury and Lauri Markkanen is questionable with a wrist injury. Miami has no players out, and Giannis Antetokounmpo is probable with a wrist injury. The Heat are the better team by a distance, and Utah is running out of healthy bodies.

Leg 2 — Orlando Magic -5.5 (-110) at Sacramento Kings. Magic (2-7) at Kings (1-9), 10:00 PM ET, Golden 1 Center. Orlando -5.5, total 222.5. The Kings are 1-9 and banged up: Domantas Sabonis is questionable with a knee injury after missing Sunday, Keegan Murray is questionable with a wrist injury, and Maxime Raynaud is out with a knee injury. Orlando is 2-7 and not exactly rolling, but the Kings are the worse team in a worse spot.

Leg 3 — New Jersey Devils at Winnipeg Jets Over 6 (-110). Devils (13-5) at Jets (7-8), 7:00 PM ET. Total 6. The Jets are without Josh Morrissey (wrist, injured reserve), Vladislav Namestnikov, Nick Bjugstad and Elias Salomonsson. The Devils are without Anthony Mantha and Seamus Casey. Two offenses that can score, two defenses missing pieces — the over is the play.

The bet

Heat -9.5 (-110) + Magic -5.5 (-110) + Devils-Jets Over 6 (-110), about +600. Three legs at -110, each one a bet worth making on its own. The Heat are laying a big number against a 1-9 team that has lost eight straight and is missing its starting point guard. The Magic are laying a small number against a 1-9 team missing its best player. And the Devils-Jets total is 6 with both defenses short-handed.

The parlay spread rule holds: only one big spread in the ticket. The Heat -9.5 is the one. The other two legs are a small spread and a total — different kinds of bets, different kinds of risk.

The honest caveat

Parlays are parlays. The Heat leg is the obvious danger — nine and a half points is a lot to cover on the road, and if Miami coasts in the fourth quarter, the back door is wide open. The Magic are 2-7 themselves, so laying points with them is a leap of faith. And the Devils-Jets over needs seven goals, which is never a guarantee. Any one leg can kill the ticket — that's the deal. But each leg clears on its own merits, and that's the only way to build one of these.

The pick

Heat -9.5 + Magic -5.5 + Devils-Jets Over 6, about +600. A $10 ticket returns about $70. Three legs, each one defensible, one big spread and two more that stand on their own.

Sports · Nov 10, 2026 · 8:37 AM ET

Wembanyama drops 40 with 9 blocks in 25 minutes — and the Spurs just keep winning

By Matt

Victor Wembanyama scored 40 points with 11 rebounds and 9 blocks in just 25 minutes as the Spurs beat the Suns 122-96 for their ninth straight win — a line only one other player in NBA history has ever produced that fast.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Victor Wembanyama played 25 minutes and 21 seconds on Monday night. That was enough for 40 points on 15-of-19 shooting, 11 rebounds, 9 blocks, 3 assists, one turnover and a plus-30. The San Antonio Spurs beat the Phoenix Suns 122-96 at Mortgage Matchup Center for their ninth straight win, moving to 9-2 — 5-0 on the road — while the Suns fell to 6-5.

Devin Booker scored 32 for Phoenix and it didn't matter. Dylan Harper added 17 points, 5 rebounds and 4 assists for San Antonio, and the Spurs never really let the Suns breathe. It was Wembanyama's third 40-point game in his first 11 appearances of the season — five days after the 52-point, 24-rebound, 8-assist night against Dallas.

Coach Mitch Johnson said afterward that Wembanyama is "allowing the game to unfold" rather than forcing it. That's a polite way of describing a 7-foot-4 player who took 19 shots in 25 minutes and made 15 of them, then blocked nine shots on the other end.

The history

The minutes are what make this one absurd. Wembanyama is the second player in NBA history to score 40 points in 25 minutes or fewer. The other was Sleepy Floyd, on February 26, 1991. That's a 35-year gap.

The blocks make it rarer still. He's the second player ever with multiple 40-point, 10-rebound, 8-block games in a single season. The first was Wilt Chamberlain. He also became the first player to post 40 points, 11 rebounds and 8-plus blocks at Mortgage Matchup Center — and per Stathead, the only other player with a 40/11/8-plus-blocks game involving the Suns was Elvin Hayes, who went for 40 points, 18 rebounds and 13 blocks against Phoenix in 1968.

Blocks have only been an official stat since 1973-74, which is worth remembering whenever a number like this shows up: the record book starts in the mid-70s, and Wembanyama is already rewriting the parts of it that involve the most complete big-man games ever played. The Spurs visit Chicago on Wednesday, and at this point the only question is what the next one looks like.

GTA 6 pre-orders are 'far above' expectations — and the download starts today

By Matt

Take-Two says GTA 6 pre-orders are tracking 'far above' initial expectations, with third-party estimates at 7.71 million digital pre-orders already — and pre-loading opens today ahead of the November 19 launch.

What happened

Take-Two Interactive reported its second-quarter fiscal 2027 results on Monday, and the headline was the one everyone was waiting for: Grand Theft Auto 6 pre-orders are tracking "far above our initial expectations," according to CEO Strauss Zelnick, who added they are "far in excess" of what the company expected and "continuing to reach unprecedented levels."

The rest of the quarter was more mixed. Net bookings came in at $1.6 billion, below the $1.62–1.67 billion guidance range, and NBA 2K27 came in below forecast despite the second-best launch in franchise history. Take-Two maintained its full-year fiscal 2027 bookings outlook of $8.0–8.2 billion and raised its revenue guidance to $7.98–8.18 billion.

Third-party estimates put a number on the demand. Sensor Tower estimates 7.71 million digital pre-orders — roughly $751 million — as of November 3, split about 5.74 million on PlayStation and 1.97 million on Xbox. Sensor Tower's Karl Kontus expects around 25 million pre-orders by launch.

What it means

The game launches Thursday, November 19, on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X|S, and pre-loading begins today, Tuesday, November 10 — the download runs up to 212 GB, so the nine-day head start is not a courtesy, it's a necessity. The launch trailer dropped October 15 with a Justice score featuring Tame Impala, SZA and King Gizzard, and Miami is getting a launch event, "GTA VI: The Festival," on Saturday, November 21 at Mana Wynwood with Cardi B on the bill, tickets distributed by lottery.

The pre-order number is the story here, not the quarter. Take-Two missed its own bookings guidance and still had its CEO talking about unprecedented demand, because the entire fiscal year now runs through one date. Nine days out, the only question left is whether the launch can hold up under the weight of the numbers already on the board.

Bills grind the Vikings 23-6 on Monday night — Buffalo's best start since 1993

By Matt

Josh Allen and James Cook ground the Vikings down in a 23-6 Monday night win — Buffalo is 7-1, its best eight-game start since 1993, while Minnesota's quarterback question just got louder.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Buffalo Bills beat the Minnesota Vikings 23-6 at U.S. Bank Stadium on Monday night, and it was every bit as one-sided as the score. Buffalo is 7-1, its best eight-game start since 1993, winners of four straight. Minnesota lost its second straight after a 6-0 start, and the Chicago Bears took over first place in the NFC North.

Josh Allen went 21-of-31 for 234 yards with a touchdown and an interception, and James Cook carried 31 times for 107 yards and a score. Keleki Latu caught a 25-yard touchdown pass just before halftime. Tyler Bass kicked three field goals, including a 52-yarder, extending his franchise-record streak to 24 straight makes. Buffalo held the ball for 42 minutes and 32 seconds and outgained Minnesota 339-178.

The Vikings' offense never got out of first gear. Carson Wentz went 8-of-25 for 81 yards with two interceptions and was benched for Max Brosmer in the fourth quarter. Minnesota finished with 178 total yards, nine first downs, 0-for-8 on third down and three turnovers. Will Reichard's 43- and 53-yard field goals were the only points of the night.

The quarterback question

This is the part that matters going forward. USA Today's takeaway from the game was blunt: Minnesota must trade for a quarterback. Wentz was starting because J.J. McCarthy is out, and the Vikings' offense has now scored 6 points in a game where the defense gave them chances to stay in it. Brosmer's two-minute drill ended at the Buffalo 13.

The Vikings were 6-0 two weeks ago. They're 6-2 now, still very much in the playoff race, but the quarterback position is the story of their season — and Monday night was the loudest evidence yet. Buffalo, meanwhile, just keeps grinding. Four straight wins, a defense that gave up 178 yards, and a running game that bled the clock dry.

Sports · Nov 10, 2026 · 8:37 AM ET

Warriors make history: first team ever to start 0-3 and win eight straight

By Matt

The Warriors crushed the Pelicans 138-87 for their eighth straight win — making them the first team in NBA history to start 0-3 and then win eight in a row.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Golden State Warriors beat the New Orleans Pelicans 138-87 at Chase Center on Monday night, and the 51-point margin was only the second-most interesting number on the board. The Warriors are now 8-3, winners of eight straight, after starting the season 0-3. No team in NBA history has ever done that before.

The last team to win seven straight after an 0-3 start was the 1950-51 Boston Celtics, in Red Auerbach's first season as coach. Golden State blew past them on Monday and kept going. It was a 6-0 homestand, capped by the fourth-largest margin of victory in franchise history and the biggest since a 157-101 win at Portland in April 2023.

Stephen Curry scored 25 points in 21 minutes with four threes. Brandin Podziemski added 25 with five threes and four assists. Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg went for 23 points, 7 rebounds and 4 assists. Derik Queen led New Orleans with 16. Jordan Poole, back in the building where he won a ring, scored 7 points on 1-of-5 shooting in 33 minutes.

The context

The Pelicans were short-handed, and that's part of the story: Zion Williamson sat for rest, Trey Murphy III is out with bilateral patellar tendinopathy, and Yves Missi is out with a knee injury. New Orleans fell to 4-7. But the Warriors' turnaround isn't about one soft opponent — it's an eight-game run that started with a team that looked broken in October and now looks like it never lost the recipe.

The injuries on Golden State's side haven't gone anywhere either. The Warriors have been winning with a thin rotation, leaning on Curry and a supporting cast that keeps filling gaps. Eight straight, a 6-0 homestand, and a piece of history nobody had ever claimed. The 0-3 start is officially ancient history.

Sports · Nov 10, 2026 · 8:36 AM ET

McDavid's assist streak ends at 18 — but the point streak hits 19, and only three players have ever done that

By Matt

Connor McDavid's assist streak ended at 18 in Edmonton's 3-1 win over the Rangers — but his point streak reached 19 games to open the season, something only Gretzky, Heatley and Sundin have ever done.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Edmonton Oilers beat the New York Rangers 3-1 at Rogers Place on Monday night, and the box score came with a plot twist. Connor McDavid scored 6:42 into the first period — unassisted, off a Dylan Garand giveaway — and his point streak reached 19 games to open the season. His assist streak, the one everyone was watching, ended at 18.

Well, almost. McDavid was briefly credited with an assist on Leon Draisaitl's empty-net goal, which would have kept the streak alive. Then the NHL pulled it off the scoresheet. Eighteen it is — tied with Adam Oates for the second-longest assist streak in NHL history, behind only Wayne Gretzky's 23 in 1990-91.

Draisaitl scored twice and now leads the NHL with 18 goals in 19 games. Devon Levi made 28 saves for the Rangers, who went 0-for-5 on the power play and got a short-handed goal from Jaroslav Chmelar. The Oilers improved to 13-3-3, first in the West, and swept the season series from New York after a 4-1 win there on November 1.

The history

The point streak is the bigger deal, and it's the one that puts McDavid in a room with three names. He has 11 goals and 37 assists — 48 points — through 19 games, making him the fourth player ever with a 19-game point streak to open a season. The other three are Wayne Gretzky, Dany Heatley and Mats Sundin.

The chase isn't over, it just changes shape. McDavid is two points from 50 on the season — only Gretzky and Mario Lemieux have ever reached 50 points in 19 games or fewer. And three assists in his next three games would tie Gretzky's mark for fastest to 40 assists in a season, 40 in 22 games. The Oilers host Philadelphia on Wednesday.

Sports · Nov 10, 2026 · 8:36 AM ET

Cooper Flagg drops 43 — the second teenager ever with a 40-10-5 game

By Matt

Cooper Flagg dropped 43 points, 10 rebounds and 6 assists as the Mavericks beat the Pacers 125-122 — becoming just the second teenager in NBA history with a 40-10-5 game, after Kevin Durant.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Cooper Flagg is 19 years old, and he just had the kind of night that makes the rest of the league check the calendar. Flagg scored a career-high 43 points on 16-of-25 shooting — 3-of-6 from three, 8-of-8 from the line — with 10 rebounds, 6 assists and 3 steals as the Dallas Mavericks beat the Indiana Pacers 125-122 at American Airlines Center on Monday night.

Thirty of the 43 came after halftime. Sixteen came in the fourth quarter, including two 18-foot pull-up jumpers in the final 62 seconds — the second a go-ahead bucket with 26.3 seconds left. Dallas trailed by 13 late in the first half and still found a way, winning its third straight to move to 7-4. It was Flagg's fourth straight double-double.

He didn't do it alone. Kyrie Irving scored 35 points on 7-of-10 from deep, and the two of them combined for 78. Pascal Siakam led Indiana with 29 — 26 of them in the first half, just 3 after the break — and the Pacers still had seven players in double figures and lost. That's the kind of night it was.

The history

The line puts Flagg in company that barely exists. He is the second teenager in NBA history to post 40 points, 10 rebounds and 5 assists in a game. The first was Kevin Durant, who went for 42 points, 13 rebounds and 6 assists for Seattle at Golden State on April 16, 2008 — the final game of the 2007-08 season. That was 18 years ago.

It was also Flagg's fifth 40-point game as a teenager, the most in NBA history, and it tied him with LeBron James for the most 35-point games by a teenager, with seven. His 14th 30-point game before turning 20 — LeBron had 20 — and per StatMuse, he's the first teenager ever with 40 points, 10 rebounds, 5 assists and 3 steals in a game. He turns 20 on December 21.

The mid-range work is the part that should scare people. Flagg has been doing his damage in tight spaces, off limited dribbles, and Monday night he closed a game from 18 feet — the shot defenses are supposed to be happy to give up. Dallas coach Dusty May called the performance unbelievable for a 19-year-old, and honestly, there isn't a better word for it.

TSL Bets parlay: Cavaliers -8.5, Spurs -8.5, Mavericks -2.5 (about +550)

By Matt

Three legs, about +550: the Cavaliers laying 8.5 against a gutted Hornets team, the Spurs laying 8.5 against a Suns team with no answer for Wembanyama, and the Mavericks laying 2.5 against a Pacers team missing its star.

The games

Cleveland Cavaliers (5-5) at Charlotte Hornets (3-7) — 7:00 PM ET, Spectrum Center, DAZN CLE

Cavaliers -8.5 (-112), total 215.5, moneyline Cavaliers -335 / Hornets +250 (BetRivers). Cleveland lost to the Knicks on Saturday and is 1-3 on the road. Charlotte is 2-4 at home and skidding.

Injuries — Cleveland: Sam Merrill (elbow) is out, Jaylon Tyson (ankle) is out, Nae'Qwan Tomlin (back) is out, and Jarrett Allen (knee contusion) is questionable.

Injuries — Charlotte: Brandon Miller (hip/groin) is out, Kon Knueppel (hamstring, reaggravated, out roughly four weeks) is out, Dennis Schroder (hamstring) is out, Grant Williams (hamstring) is out, and Moussa Diabate (quad) is questionable. The Hornets are missing their leading scorer (Miller, 20.6 a game), their point guard (Schroder, a team-high 4.9 assists), and their rookie (Knueppel) all at once.

San Antonio Spurs (8-2) at Phoenix Suns (3-5) — 9:00 PM ET, Mortgage Matchup Center, DAZN-SAS

Spurs -8.5 (-115), total 227.5, moneyline Spurs -352 / Suns +282 (PickDawgz). San Antonio is unbeaten on the road at 4-0 with a +14.8 point differential away from home, and arrives on a five-game winning run. Phoenix has won three games all season.

Injuries — San Antonio: Tobias Harris (calf) is out, Jayden Quaintance (knee) is out, and Devin Vassell (adductor tightness) is questionable. Victor Wembanyama is averaging 29.1 points and 12.0 rebounds, and he scored 29 in just 24 minutes in his last game.

Injuries — Phoenix: Mark Williams is out after shoulder surgery, leaving undersized Oso Ighodaro and developing Khaman Maluach to cover all 48 minutes at center against Wembanyama. Jordan Goodwin (calf) is questionable, and Rasheer Fleming (ankle) is questionable.

Indiana Pacers (3-7) at Dallas Mavericks (6-4) — 8:00 PM ET, American Airlines Center, DAZN-IND

Mavericks -2.5 (-117), total 222.5, moneyline Mavericks -155 / Pacers +130 (PickDawgz). Dallas has won two straight after blowing out Brooklyn on Sunday. Indiana is 3-2 on the road but is one of the most injured teams in the league.

Injuries — Indiana: Tyrese Haliburton (hamstring) is out, Ben Sheppard (meniscus) is out, Johnny Furphy (ACL) is out, and T.J. McConnell (hamstring) is questionable. Haliburton averages 21.8 points a game and has played just four times this season.

Injuries — Dallas: Dereck Lively II (foot) is out, Sergio de Larrea (knee) is out, and Tarik Biberovic (heel) is out. Daniel Gafford is expected to be limited to 20-25 minutes, and Caleb Martin is available despite a thumb issue.

The legs

Leg 1 — Cavaliers -8.5 (-112). Charlotte is missing four rotation players, including its best scorer, its starting point guard, and its rookie — and Cleveland's net rating (+2.6) sits against Charlotte's (-14.2). The Hornets are converting just 28.8 percent from three, one of the worst marks in the league, and they have nobody left to create offense. Cleveland has to win by nine. Against this version of Charlotte, that is the right side.

Leg 2 — Spurs -8.5 (-115). The matchup is the whole bet: Phoenix has no center. Williams is out after shoulder surgery, and the Suns have to cover 48 minutes at the five against Wembanyama — 29.1 points and 12 rebounds a game, first in the league in blocks at 3.9 a night — with Ighodaro and a rookie. San Antonio is 4-0 on the road and has covered this number in four of its last five. The Spurs' three guards break down Phoenix's perimeter defense, and nobody on the Suns can challenge Wembanyama at the rim.

Leg 3 — Mavericks -2.5 (-117). The Pacers are missing their star, their backup point guard is questionable, and they just flew home from a loss in Mexico City on Saturday. Dallas is at home, has won two straight, and just blew out Brooklyn by 37 on Sunday — pulling its starters early, which matters on the second night of a back-to-back. The Mavericks have covered in seven of their last eight as favorites against Eastern Conference opponents. Two and a half points, at home, against a team that cannot score enough without Haliburton.

The honest caveat

Every leg has a real reason to lose, and the record says so. The Cavaliers are 3-7 against the spread this season and have failed to cover in nine of their last ten as road favorites against Eastern Conference opponents — and the Hornets have covered seven of their last eight against Cleveland following a loss. The Spurs are 4-6 ATS, the home team has covered seven of the last eight in the Spurs-Suns matchup, and San Antonio has failed to cover in each of its last four games at the Mortgage Matchup Center. The Mavericks are on the second night of a back-to-back, Gafford is limited, and the road team has covered the last six games between these two. Three favorites, three real risks. Nothing here is a lock — that is why it pays about +550 instead of -110.

The pick

3-leg parlay, about +550: Cavaliers -8.5 (-112) + Spurs -8.5 (-115) + Mavericks -2.5 (-117). A $10 ticket returns about $65. Three favorites, three injury-ravaged opponents, three different reasons to believe. That is the bet.

TSL Bets: Oilers-Rangers over 6.5 (-110) — the league's best offense meets a backup goalie

By Matt

Connor McDavid goes for his 19th straight game with an assist tonight, the Oilers own the league's best attack, and the Rangers are without Igor Shesterkin — out for the season after knee surgery. Two teams that score, one goalie situation that doesn't scare anyone. Over 6.5 (-110) is the play.

The game

New York Rangers (10-7-1) at Edmonton Oilers (12-3-3) — 8:00 PM ET, Rogers Place, ESPN+

Oilers -156, total 6.5, moneyline Oilers -156 / Rangers +130 (FanDuel). The over is -112 at FanDuel and -110 on the consensus board. The Oilers are the No. 1 team in the Pacific at 12-3-3. The Rangers are No. 3 in the Metro at 10-7-1, on a three-game winning streak — but 1-5-1 on the road. The computer projection is 6.6 goals.

Injuries — Edmonton: Ryan Nugent-Hopkins is day-to-day after taking a puck to the head in Saturday's win over Vancouver — coach Mike Babcock said the initial fear was a broken jaw, but Nugent-Hopkins seemed to be doing OK and Babcock does not expect him to miss much time. Isaac Howard (knee) and Frederik Andersen (knee) are on injured reserve, along with Mattias Janmark, Alec Regula, and Matt Savoie. Zach Hyman returned Saturday after five games out with a neck injury.

Injuries — New York: Igor Shesterkin is on injured reserve after season-ending knee surgery — the Rangers' star goalie is done for the year, and Joonas Korpisalo, acquired from Boston on July 1, has yet to play this season because of a lower-body injury. Sean Durzi (lower body) is on injured reserve, Joe Veleno (lower body) is out long-term, and Alberts Smits is day-to-day.

Storylines: The headliner is McDavid. He has an assist in all 18 games Edmonton has played, tied with Adam Oates for the second-longest assist streak in NHL history, and tonight is game 19 — five away from Wayne Gretzky's record of 23. The Oilers are the league's highest-scoring team at over four goals a game, and McDavid (47 points) and Draisaitl (16 goals) are the two hottest players in the sport. Edmonton beat the Rangers 4-1 in New York on November 1, the night McDavid became the fastest player ever to reach 30 assists in a season. The Rangers have won three straight, including a 4-3 win at Seattle on Saturday, but their road record says they are a different team away from the Garden — and now they are doing it without their franchise goalie.

The bet

Oilers-Rangers over 6.5 (-110). This is a goals bet, not a side bet, and the matchup points one way. Edmonton scores more than anyone in the league. The Rangers score plenty themselves (over three a game) and are now defending with a backup goalie situation against the best attack in hockey. The total has sat at 6.5 all day, the models project 6.6, and the Oilers have been over four goals a game as a team. McDavid chasing history at home, in front of a crowd that knows what is on the line, is not the kind of night that ends 2-1.

The honest caveat

Totals at 6.5 are coin flips by nature, and the Rangers have won three straight by playing tighter hockey. If Nugent-Hopkins sits, Edmonton loses a top-six piece — though Babcock sounds optimistic. And the Rangers' road record is bad, but bad road teams sometimes play exactly one good defensive game, and this would be a bad night for it to happen. The over needs seven goals. Both teams are capable of scoring four on their own. That is the bet.

The pick

Over 6.5 (-110). A $10 ticket returns about $19.10. The league's best offense, a backup goalie, and a record chase at home.

New Orleans Pelicans (4-6) at Golden State Warriors (7-3) — 10:00 PM ET, Chase Center, NBA TV

Golden State is 5-1 at home this season.

Warriors -9.5 (-102). Nine and a half is a big number, and this is the bet anyway. The Warriors are 7-3, have won seven straight, are 5-1 at home, and have the best player on the floor by a distance. And the Pelicans can genuinely score: 120.5 points a game, Zion at 9-of-9 on Sunday, Murphy and Fears both going for 25-plus. If this turns into a shootout, the back door is wide open. But the spot — home, rested, rolling, against a tired team that cannot defend — is the best one on tonight's board.

The pick

Warriors -9.5 (-102). A $10 ticket returns about $19.80. Seven straight wins, a rested Curry, and a Pelicans team on the second night of a back-to-back after giving up 139.

TSL Bets: Vikings +3 (-102) — the home dog with the No. 2 defense on Monday night

By Matt

Two 6-1 teams meet on Monday Night Football, and the home dog is getting a field goal. The Vikings have the No. 2 defense in the league, Carson Wentz has already won games for this team, and Buffalo's offensive line is banged up. Vikings +3 (-102) is the play.

The game

Buffalo Bills (6-1) at Minnesota Vikings (6-1) — 8:15 PM ET, U.S. Bank Stadium, ESPN/ABC

Bills -3 (-118), total 44, moneyline Bills -175 / Vikings +145 (BetMGM, via USA TODAY Sportsbook Wire). Buffalo is 5-2 against the spread and 5-2 to the over this season. Minnesota is 4-3 ATS and just 1-6 to the over — this defense keeps games low-scoring. The total opened at 46.5 and has been bet down to 44, which tells you what the market expects: a tight, low-scoring game. Tight, low-scoring games are where home dogs getting a field goal make their money.

Injuries — Buffalo: WR Josh Palmer (hamstring) is out. LT Dion Dawkins (rib) and OL Alec Anderson (ankle) are questionable after missing Thursday and Friday practices, and DT Greg Gaines (calf) and WR Tyrell Shavers (knee) are questionable too. The Bills' injury report has swollen to 21 players, and the worst of it is on the offensive line and in the receiver room.

Injuries — Minnesota: QB Kyler Murray (hamstring) is out, so Carson Wentz starts. WR Jauan Jennings (calf) is out. S Theo Jackson (quad) and S Jay Ward (knee) are questionable. RB Demond Claiborne (knee) is out.

Storylines: Two first-place teams, both 6-1, and the whole game turns on the quarterback change. Murray is week-to-week with the hamstring, and Wentz — who helped the Vikings win in relief in Week 1 and started the rain-affected game at Chicago the week after — gets the start. The Vikings' defense is No. 2 in the league in EPA per play, and it has carried them all season: they are 6-1 despite shaky quarterback play. Buffalo's defense, by contrast, ranks 26th in EPA per play and sits in the bottom 10 in passing yards allowed. The Bills got to 6-1 on a soft schedule and some big injuries to opponents. Minnesota is coming off its first loss of the season, 19-15 at Detroit. Buffalo blasted Baltimore 36-10 on November 1.

The bet

Vikings +3 (-102, BetMGM). The home dog, getting a field goal, with the No. 2 defense in football, against a road favorite whose offensive line is held together with tape and whose defense is one of the league's worst by EPA. Wentz is not the reason to bet Minnesota — the defense is. If this game lands where the total says it will, in the low 40s, three points is a lot of points. The Vikings have already won games with Wentz under center this season. They do not need him to be great. They need him to not lose it, and let the defense and the home crowd do the rest.

The honest caveat

Wentz is a backup for a reason, and Buffalo has Josh Allen. If the Bills' banged-up line holds up for one night, Allen is capable of winning this game by more than a field goal on his own — and the Vikings' offense, without Murray's legs, has struggled to sustain drives all season. The Bills are 5-2 ATS for a reason, too. Nothing here is a lock. But at home, with that defense, getting three? That is the side.

The pick

Vikings +3 (-102). A $10 ticket returns about $19.80. Two 6-1 teams, one field goal, and the better defense is the one getting the points.

Collectibles · Nov 9, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

Magic's Star Trek Secret Lair superdrop beams in today — five drops, $29.99 each, and a sixth still to come

By Matt

Secret Lair x Star Trek: Set Phasers to Superdrop goes on sale today at noon ET on MagicSecretLair.com — five drops at $29.99 non-foil or $39.99 foil, from full-art lands to a full Klingon-language set, with a sixth drop still unrevealed and the main set landing Friday.

What happened

The transporters are warmed up. Secret Lair x Star Trek: Set Phasers to Superdrop goes on sale today, Monday, November 9, at 9 a.m. PT / noon ET on MagicSecretLair.com, with a pre-queue opening an hour earlier. Wizards of the Coast revealed five of the six drops on October 27. The sixth is still to be revealed.

Each drop costs $29.99 in non-foil or $39.99 in foil, while supplies last. All five revealed drops run $149.95 non-foil or $199.95 foil before tax and shipping, and spending $199.99 earns a foil copy of Return of the Wildspeaker, reskinned as "Transporter Boondoggle." Orders over $99 ship free.

The five drops

New Worlds — full-art basic lands from new worlds, the kind of drop that ends up in decks for years after the set itself is forgotten.

Beam Me Up — the transporter drop, and the cleverest of the five: four reversible cards. Eerie Interlude becomes Beam Us Up, Momentary Blink, Essence Flux becomes Materialize, and Conjurer's Closet becomes The Transporter. Every card in the drop is reversible.

tlhIngan maH — a full Klingon-language set, with Klingon-script cards developed with help from Marc Okrand, the linguist who created the language for the films. This is the one collectors will be talking about longest.

KHAAAAAAAAN — the Star Trek II: The Wrath of Khan drop, which Wizards itself notes knows exactly how many A's belong in the title.

Lower Decks — the chaos of Star Trek: Lower Decks, for the people who want their Magic cards to be funny.

Why the timing matters

The superdrop lands four days before the main event: Magic: The Gathering | Star Trek hits tabletop on Friday, November 13, with prereleases running November 6–12 and the MTG Arena release on November 10. All four Commander decks have been revealed, each a ready-to-play 100-card deck with 32 new-to-Magic cards. The set is the next Universes Beyond release, and Wizards is treating it like one — a Secret Lair superdrop, a full Commander lineup, and a set code (TRK) collectors already know by heart.

The business behind it is worth a line, too. Hasbro reported that Magic: The Gathering set a quarterly revenue record of $616.1 million in Q3 2026, up 34 percent year over year, with The Hobbit as the fourth-best-selling set in Magic history and the highest prerelease participation ever for a Universes Beyond set. Star Trek is walking into the biggest moment the game has ever had.

What to watch

Secret Lair sales are queue-based and while-supplies-last, and the pre-queue opens at 8 a.m. PT — an hour before the sale. Adding a drop to your basket does not reserve it, so the drops that sell out will sell out fast, and the Klingon set is the obvious candidate. The unrevealed sixth drop is the other thing to watch: Wizards has not said what it is or when it appears, and a surprise sixth drop in a superdrop like this has a way of becoming the chase card of the whole release.

Set phasers, indeed.

The details

Sale opens: Today, Nov 9 — 9 a.m. PT / noon ET

Pre-queue: 8 a.m. PT / 11 a.m. ET

Where: MagicSecretLair.com

Price: $29.99 non-foil / $39.99 foil per drop

Bonus: Foil "Transporter Boondoggle" per $199.99 spent

Main set: Nov 13 (tabletop), Nov 10 (Arena)

Gaming · Nov 9, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

GTA 6 takes over Selfridges in London — 60-plus items, including a £2,500 jacket

By Matt

Selfridges has turned its Bond Street store in London into a Grand Theft Auto 6 takeover, with more than 60 items of merch on sale — including a £2,500 jacket. The game itself lands November 19, and pre-loading opens Tuesday.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

What happened

Grand Theft Auto 6 has taken over one of the most famous department stores in the world. Selfridges has opened a GTA 6 activation at its Bond Street store in London, and put the merchandise on sale online on Monday morning, according to VGC. The range runs to more than 60 items — including a £2,500 jacket.

It is the kind of takeover only a game this size gets. GTA 6 is ten days from release, and Rockstar is now putting it in front of people who do not follow gaming news at all: shoppers on Oxford Street, walking past a storefront that has been turned into Leonida.

The countdown is real now

The timing is not an accident. Pre-loading for GTA 6 opens on Tuesday, November 10, and Rockstar has revealed the game's file size ahead of launch. The game itself arrives November 19 on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X/S — a date that has already moved twice, and one the whole industry has been planning around for months. Last week brought the launch trailer, a month out from release, and the final piece of Rockstar's slow rollout: Trailer 1 in December 2023, Trailer 2 in May 2025, a 26-minute extended look in August, and the launch trailer on October 15.

The Selfridges takeover is the physical version of the same rollout. Sixty-plus items is not a capsule — it is a full merch line, with a £2,500 jacket at the top of it. That price tells you who Rockstar thinks is buying: not just players, but collectors who treat GTA merch the way sneakerheads treat a limited drop.

Watch the launch trailer

Rockstar dropped the final trailer exactly one month before launch. Two minutes, Jason's confession up front, and every shot captured on PS5.

Watch on YouTube

Source: Grand Theft Auto VI Launch Trailer — Rockstar Games on YouTube. The official launch trailer: Jason and Lucia, one month out from November 19.

What it means

A department-store takeover in central London is not how most games launch. It is how the biggest entertainment launch of the year launches. GTA 6 has been the story of the industry for three years without ever being playable, and now it is ten days away, with pre-loading opening tomorrow and a £2,500 jacket sitting in a Selfridges window. The game does not need the marketing. The marketing is the point — Rockstar is making sure that by November 19, there is not a person in London who does not know what GTA 6 is.

GTA VI arrives November 19 on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X/S. Pre-order the PS5 version here.
